股價相對大盤走勢

台玻(1802 TT) Buy

破曉巨浪已然凝聚。 
投資建議：高階玻纖布 Low Dk2/Low CTE出貨動能攀升，帶動
3Q26 毛利率季增逾 8%， 10/2026 營收創高。研究部同步上修
4Q26、 2027 年獲利預估。預估台玻 2027 年稅後 EPS 4.84 元。
給予投資建議 Buy，目標價 100 元 (2027 年 PBR3.97X)。  
3Q26 毛利率再創高至 31.60%：台玻 3Q26 受惠於 Low Dk2 、
Low CTE 出貨比重拉升，3Q26 毛利率較 2Q26 提升 8.27%至
31.60%，亦優於原預期之 26.23%。 3Q26 稅後純益 27.05 億元、
稅後 EPS 0.93 元，均優於預期。 
10/2026 營收 MoM+12.04%、 YoY+56.49%：台玻 10/2026 營收
55.66 億元，MoM+12.04%、YoY+56.49%，優於預期，主因玻纖
布事業營收持續攀升，整體出貨動能較 09/2026 成長。以研究
部預期當前玻纖布營收比重約 40~46%，高階玻纖布占其中
40~42%推估，10/2026 高階玻纖布營收貢獻 MoM+13~16%。  
預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 比重仍高於 Low CTE：台玻高階玻纖布
以 Low Dk2/Low CTE 為主要營收來源。Low CTE 已於 2025 年
底開始小量出貨，目前產品出貨量已較 2026 年初顯著增加，預
期營收佔比將於 2027 年持續提升，並於 2H27~2028 年新產能
開出後，追趕 Low Dk2 。預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 營收比重仍將
高於 Low Dk1 及 Low CTE。  
積極擴建 Low Dk/Low CTE產能，期待擴產效益顯現，預估 2027
年稅後 EPS 4.84 元：11/02/2026 董事會通過桃園廠、鹿港廠窯
爐增建及子公司台嘉蚌埠玻璃纖維有限公司冷修擴充案，預估
投入金額約新台幣 16.3 億元及人民幣 11.5 億元。Low CTE 產
品已取得國內載板大廠認證，2028 年訂單洽談順暢。研究部預
估台玻 2027 年營收 711.51 億元，YoY+33.24%，稅後純益 140.68
億元，YoY+69.11%，稅後 EPS 4.84 元。

2 群益投顧 
  台玻 ESG： 
ESG 方面，台玻堅守四大主軸，包括強化永續環境、落實誠信經營、關懷社
會公益及發展友善職場。強化永續環境方面，台玻在符合法規條件下，積極
減降生產過程對環境的外部成本，並妥善安置生產後產生之廢棄物；落實誠
信經營方面，台玻始終秉持該核心經營理念，與對股東、員工及利害關係人
之承諾及責任；關懷社會公益方面，透過參與社會公益活動，落實回饋社會
及關懷責任，並提供員工及經銷商子女相關獎學金，培育人才；發展友善職
場方面，尊重人權及性別平等，提供員工舒適且安全職場環境。 
公司簡介： 
台玻成立於 1964 年，事業群分別平板事業部、纖維事業部、容器事業部、
汽車玻璃事業部。主要產品包括平板玻璃、玻璃纖維及玻璃器皿，生產基地
位於台灣及中國兩地。平板玻璃產品均已通過歐盟 CE、美國 ANSI、台灣
CNS 相關規範，低輻射 Low-E 複層玻能維持可見光情況下，阻擋紅外線熱
能進入室內，具良好隔熱效果，主要用於建築物玻璃帷幕牆。公司也有發展
玻璃基板，有望搭上 CoPoS 題材。玻璃纖維方面，台玻有複合材料用的 FRP
及玻璃纖維布。FRP 玻璃纖維具備優異耐腐蝕耐候性、低成本及高強度特
性。玻璃纖維布廣泛運用於 5G 伺服器、AI 伺服器、手機平板及筆電等產
品。 
圖一、台玻產品組合(營收比重)

資料來源：台玻，群益投顧預估彙整 
台玻 2026 年高階玻纖布市占率上看 45%， 2028 年訂單洽談順暢： 
台玻從 1990 年開始生產玻纖布，技術並非一蹴可磯，而是多年人才培育及
技術經驗累積推敲而得。最開始是與美國 Owens Corning 簽署技術合作授
權，從中取得全球最先進玻璃纖維漿料配方及技術指導，並向日本 Kanebo
購入織布技術。台玻亦為繼 Nittobo、 Ibiden 後，第三家廠商具備自行研發
量產 Low Dk 玻璃纖維布廠商，並已通過國內外大廠認證，於 2025 年陸續
出貨。主要應用於 5G 基礎建設、高階伺服器、高階顯卡及車用等領域。 
AI 投資強勁推升載板加速升級，Low Dk1、 Low Dk2 玻纖布及 Low CTE 玻
纖布需求隨之暴漲。現階段台玻產能已達滿載，且訂單能見度至 2027 年，
2028 年訂單洽談順暢。公司表示，2026 年目標高階玻纖布市占率 40%~45%。

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

3 群益投顧 
台玻客戶需求達 2028 年，Nittobo 指出 2029 年度產能擴增至 2025 年度之
3.6 倍： 
台玻 T-glass 已通過國內外廠商認證，Ibiden 亦表示 T-glass 仍處於供不應
求階段。T-glass 尚待日本大廠 Nittobo 於 2H27 後開出新產能，才得以部分
緩解，且考量技術門檻偏高、窯爐建置期長，產能放量亦需時間等待。台玻
董事長更於近日指出，客戶需求已達 2028 年，當前公司供給仍追不上需求。
Nittobo 亦再次表示 T-glass 需求強勁。Nittobo 目前產線均處滿載生產階段，
且決定於台灣工廠增建玻纖絲製造熔爐，並於日本福島增設玻纖布生產工
廠。雖 Nittobo 於 2028 年度產能將為 2025 年度之 3 倍，惟觀察產品需求已
非當前擴產速度得以消化。Nittobo 於 11/05/2026 財報說明會上表示，將投
資 200 億日元，擴充日本福島縣與台灣營運據點之生產設備，增產 T-glass，
目標 2029 年度產能擴增至 2025 年度之 3.6 倍。 
產品報價方面，富喬於 06/26/2026 調漲 E 布報價 30%。大陸 CCL 龍頭廠建
滔積層板 04/28/2026 發布漲價通知，宣布即日起 FR-4 銅箔基板、半固化片
（ PP）將再調漲 10%。 05/27/2026 再調漲板料報價 10%、 PP 調漲 20%。
07/06/2026 分別再次調漲 FR-4、CEM-1/22F 及 PP 等產品報價約 15%、10%、
15%。08/28/2026 分別調漲 FR-4、7628(含 )以上厚布、7628 以下薄布約 10%、
10%、 20%。為因應市場強勁需求，富喬、建滔積層板 08/2026 後仍持續調
漲產品報價。 
3Q26 毛利率再創高，QoQ+8.27%至 31.60%：  
台玻 3Q26 受惠於 Low Dk2 、 Low CTE 等高毛利產品出貨比重拉升，帶動
3Q26 營業毛利 QoQ+58.80%、 YoY+257.07%，且毛利率較 2Q26 提升 8.27%
至 31.60%，亦優於原預期之 26.23%。因此 3Q26 稅後純益 27.05 億元、稅
後 EPS 0.93 元，均優於原預期之 20.47 億元、0.70 元。1~3Q26 台玻累計營
收達 364.41 億元，YoY+18.69%，毛利率 25.38%，稅後純益 45.66 億元，
YoY 由虧轉盈，稅後 EPS 1.57 元。 
10/2026 營收 MoM+12.04%、 YoY+56.49%：  
台玻 10/2026 營收 55.66 億元，MoM+12.04%、 YoY+56.49%，優於預期，與
原預期值 52.21 億元，差異約+6.2%。主因玻纖布事業營收持續攀升，整體
出貨動能較 09/2026 成長，部分抵銷中國區域平板玻璃尚未損平影響。以研
究部預期當前玻纖布營收比重約 40~46%，高階玻纖布占其中 40~42%推估，
10/2026 高階玻纖布營收貢獻約 MoM+13~16%。  
不只有 Low Dk/Low CTE， Q 布也為台玻戰略儲備： 
2027~2028 年伴隨 AI 伺服器升級及機櫃出貨增加下，CCL 板材有望大量導
入 M9 等高階材料，並搭配 Q 布或 Low Dk2 等混合配方，且台玻亦具 Q 布
接單出貨能力。 
日前台玻參展 TPCA Show，展出 Q 布玻纖紗，顯示其 Q 布技術儲備，大秀
高階肌肉。惟觀察 AI 伺服器規格調整，預期 2027 年產品需求仍以 Low Dk
系列玻纖布為主，大量搭載 Q 布尚早。不同材料規格之玻纖布，其製程方
式雖雷同，然配方均有差異，窯爐使用亦不同，故產能簽訂後，供應鏈替換
廠商可能性不高。預期台玻將視下游廠商產品需求，決議 Q 布生產時程。

### Page 4

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

4 群益投顧 
預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 比重仍高於 Low CTE：  
台玻 E-glass 轉至中國產銷，當前高階玻纖布以 Low Dk2/Low CTE 為主要
營收來源。Low CTE 已於 2025 年底開始小量出貨，目前產品出貨量已較
2026 年初顯著增加，預期營收佔比將於 2027 年持續提升，並於 2H27~2028
年新產能開出後，追趕 Low Dk2。目前下游終端需求仍以 Low Dk 產品為主
力，預期 2027 年 Low Dk2 營收比重仍將高於 Low Dk1 及 Low CTE。  
積極擴建 Low Dk/Low CTE 產能，期待擴產效益顯現，預估 2027 年稅後
EPS 4.84 元： 
產能布局方面，05/11/2026 經董事會宣布，為因應市場對於高階玻纖布需求
上揚，新增資本支出 22.42 億元，用以擴建公司桃園廠及鹿港廠窯爐及冷修
案，預計主要用於增加 Low Dk2、 T-glass 產能、提高市佔率，最快 2Q27 投
產。06/12/2026 股東會董事長指出，2026 年預計再增加 20 億元資本支出，
用於擴充 Low Dk(低介電損耗)、Low CTE(低熱膨脹係數)之 T 布產能，並表
示產品供不應求將延續至 2027 年底。目前高階玻纖布擴產計畫共分三期，
第一期已於 2026 年投入並量產，第二期預計 2026 年完成，新增之 20 億元
為第三期投資計畫，預計 2027 年完工。11/02/2026 董事會通過桃園廠、鹿
港廠窯爐增建及子公司台嘉蚌埠玻璃纖維有限公司冷修擴充案，預估投入
金額約新台幣 16.3 億元及人民幣 11.5 億元。 
產品報價方面，台玻始終秉持於維繫良好客戶關係，並以客戶需求為營運宗
旨。雖 2025~2026 年以來 E-glass 報價倍數增長，惟基於多年來與客戶合作
建立之信任及當前產品利潤貢獻尚屬可控範圍，故而預期將不急於大幅反
映產品報價，但亦不排除部分調整產品價格。 
預期 2027 年 Low Dk2/Low CTE玻纖布出貨量仍將較 2026 年提升，Low Dk1
營收貢獻亦將延續動能。台玻 Low CTE 產品已取得國內載板大廠認證，訂
單能見度已達 2027 年，且 2028 年訂單洽談順暢。若供應鏈產品需求無較大
質變，2028 年玻纖布事業群仍將維持成長格局。預估台玻 4Q26 底 ~2027 年
玻纖布營收比重可望超越平板玻璃及玻璃器皿。研究部預估台玻 2026 年營
收 534.01 億元，YoY+28.70%，毛利率 28.28%，稅後純益 83.19 億元，YoY
虧轉盈，稅後 EPS 2.86 元；2027 年營收 711.51 億元，YoY+33.24%，毛利
率 34.43%，稅後純益 140.68 億元，YoY+69.11%，稅後 EPS 4.84 元。 
圖二、台玻季營收變化預估

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 11 月 16 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 黃仁勳請辭衝擊輝達，AI 需求韌性支撐科技股反彈：輝達執行長黃仁勳因涉及國安疑慮的相
關爭議，11/12 正式辭任執行長及董事職務，由財務長 Colette Kress 暫代，輝達當日股價盤
中一度重挫近 12%，惟隨後跌幅收斂收跌 4.3%。領導層更迭引發市場對公司策略及 AI 產業
發展的疑慮，但 CoreWeave 第三季營收年增 168%，因受惠 AI 算力需求強勁，此一利多顯
示 AI 需求尚未顯著轉弱，惟輝達管理團隊穩定性、後續財報及政策調查進展，仍是影響科技

股評價的重要變數。
◼ 美國 10 月通膨高於預期，Fed 升息預期推升美債殖利率：美國 11/10 公布 10 月 CPI 月增
0.35%，高於預期的 0.3%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，亦高於預期的 0.2%，顯示通膨壓力仍具黏
性。因 2025 年政府停擺造成資料缺漏，本次未公布年增率。Fed 理事 Waller 表示，若核心
通膨維持 0.3%左右，12 月應考慮升息 2 碼，市場因此提高對後續緊縮的預期。
◼ 伊朗上週末再度對美軍設施進行攻擊，惟美方尚無反擊行動：美國國務卿魯比歐 11/13 表示，
美國對伊朗造成的經濟壓力已大到沒有必要採取軍事行動。不過，伊朗革命衛隊上週末以無人
機攻擊美軍在巴林的雷達站，但美軍尚無進一步反擊，留意之後美方是否仍以封鎖取代攻擊。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短期審慎、中期仍正向，AI 供應鏈基本面持續具支撐：黃仁勳辭任使輝達面臨管理層
接班、企業治理及政策調查等不確定性，加上 Fed 升息預期及 AI 資料中心監管疑慮，市場
短線波動可能延續。然而，該事件並未直接改變 AI 算力需求，且上週 CoreWeave Q3 財報

營收年增 168%、台積電 10 月營收年增 59.7%，均顯示 AI 基礎建設投資仍具動能。整體對
科技股維持短期審慎、但中長期基本面仍在，台灣及日本等具技術競爭優勢、獲利與現金流
穩健的半導體及 AI 供應鏈亦值得留意。惟市場焦點已由資本支出規模轉向投資報酬與現金
流，後續仍須關注輝達財報及管理團隊能否維持客戶信心。
◼ Fed 升息預期壓抑債市，中天期美債及投資級債仍相對具吸引力： 美國 10 月 CPI 高於預
期，加上 Fed 官員偏鷹表態，使債券市場持續承受政策利率上修壓力。美國 10 年期公債殖
利率重回 5%以上，收益水準已具吸引力。相較之下，短天期債券雖然價格波動較小，但殖
利率較低，且未來仍面臨政策利率及再投資風險；超長天期債券則易受到財政赤字、發債供
給與期限溢價影響。中天期美債在收益與利率風險之間相對均衡，維持審慎正向看法。信用
債方面，投資級債收益條件仍具支撐，惟須留意高資本支出企業的融資與現金流風險。

2026年 11月 20日 全6頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 10月全国消費者物価 
電気代などエネルギーが上昇に転じ、コアCPIは伸び率拡大

経済調査部 エコノミスト 横田 凱

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 10月の全国コア CPI（除く生鮮食品）は前年比＋2.2％と前月から伸び率が拡
大した。資源高が波及し電気代やガス代が大きく上昇したことでエネルギーの前年比
伸び率がプラスに転じた。一方、生鮮食品やエネルギーを除いた全国新コアコア CPI
は同＋2.2％と前月から伸び率が縮小した。コメ価格の下落の影響で非耐久消費財（除
く生鮮食品・エネルギー）の伸びが減速したことが主因だ。 
◼ コア CPIの前年比の動きを財・サービス別に見ると、エネルギーの伸び率がプラスに
転じたほか、耐久消費財の伸び率が拡大した。非耐久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネル
ギー）、サービスの伸び率は縮小した。半耐久消費財の伸び率は横ばいだった。 
◼ 先行きの物価上昇率について、コア CPI上昇率は 26年度末にかけて前年比＋3％超ま
で高まると見込んでいる。原油高が、エネルギーだけでなく、幅広い財・サービスの
価格へ波及するとみられる。賃上げの価格転嫁と併せ、基調的な物価への上昇圧力と
なるだろう。27 年度は、こうした物価上昇圧力は継続する一方で、飲食料品にかかる
消費税率の引き下げにより、コアCPIの上昇率は縮小するとみられる。

### Page 2

1

2 / 6

26年10月 CPI：エネルギーが再びプラスになり、コア CPI上昇率は拡大 
2026年 10月の全国コア CPI（除く生鮮食品）は前年比＋2.2％と前月から伸び率が拡大した
（図表1）。3-5カ月前の原油価格等を参照する電気代やガス代が大きく上昇し、政策による押し
下げ効果を上回ったことで、エネルギー（9月：同▲0.6％→10月：同＋2.2％）が 3カ月ぶり
にプラスに転じたことが主因だ。なお、政府のエネルギー高対策による押し下げの効果は依然
として大きく、政策要因1を除いたコアCPIの伸び率は当社の試算で同＋3.6％程度である2。 
一方、全国新コアコア CPI（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）（前年比＋2.2％）の伸び率は前月
から縮小した。コメ価格の下落などにより、生鮮食品を除く食料の伸びが減速したことで、非
耐久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）の伸び率が縮小した。

小・中学校の給食費無償化、水道料減免を指す。

### Page 3

1

3 / 6

原油高が反映され電気代や都市ガス代が上昇し、エネルギーの伸び率がプラスに転換 
コア CPIの前年比の動きを財・サービス別に見ると（図表 3）、エネルギー（前年比＋2.2％）
の伸び率がプラスに転じたほか、耐久消費財（同＋4.1％）の伸び率が拡大した。一方、非耐
久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）（同＋2.8％）やサービス（同＋1.4％）の伸び率が縮
小した。また、半耐久消費財（同＋3.1％）の伸び率は前月から横ばいだった。 
エネルギーに含まれる 5品目はいずれも前月から伸び率が拡大した。とりわけ、11カ月ぶり
にプラスに転じた電気代（9月：前年比▲2.4％→10月：同＋1.6％）や、都市ガス代（9月：
同＋0.2％→10月：同＋5.3％）の寄与度が大きい。それぞれ、政府による補助額は前年同月よ
り多かったものの、今年夏ごろの資源高を反映した価格の上昇がそれを上回った。 
耐久消費財では、携帯電話機（9月：前年比＋10.9％→10月：同＋17.2％）やパソコン（ノ
ート型）（9月：同＋0.8％→10月：同＋2.7％）など、電子機器の伸び率が拡大した。 
一方、非耐久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）では、うるち米Ｂ（9 月：前年比▲
21.1％→10月：同▲29.8％）やうるち米Ａ（9月：同▲19.6％→10月：同▲29.4％）など、コ
メ価格を中心に一部の食料品の下落幅が拡大した。 
サービスでは、通信料（携帯電話）（9月：前年比＋1.6％→10月：同＋0.4％）や自動車保険
料（任意）（9月：同＋5.9％→10月：同＋4.6％）などで、前年同月の値上げの裏の影響が表れ
たことなどから伸び率が縮小した。 
また、半耐久消費財では、マット（9月：前年比▲0.0％→10月：同＋3.5％）や女性用セー
ター（長袖）（9月：同▲0.2％→10月：同＋2.7％）などの伸び率がプラスに転じた一方、自動
車タイヤ（9月：同＋1.4％→10 月：同＋0.4％）やメモリーカード（9月：同＋152.0％→10
月：同＋107.9％）などの伸び率が縮小した。

先行き：原油高などの波及で、コア CPIは26年度末にかけて＋3％超を予想 
先行きの物価上昇率について、コア CPI上昇率は 26年度末にかけて前年比＋3％超まで高ま
るとみている。当面は、原油高の幅広い財・サービスへの波及が物価を押し上げる主因となる
だろう。また、賃金と物価の循環的な上昇も続くとみられる。 
原油価格の動向には注意が必要だ。米国・イスラエルとイランの間で断続的な攻撃の応酬は
続いており、中東の和平に向けた先行きはいまだ不透明である。原油価格の代表的な指標であ
る WTI先物価格は 11月 19日終値で 83ドル/バレル程度と、中東情勢の悪化前よりも高い状況
が続いている。 
足元の原油高は、直接的にエネルギー価格を押し上げる。電気・ガスの燃料費調整制度で
は、3-5カ月前のエネルギー資源の平均輸入価格が料金に反映されるため、原油高がラグを伴
って波及し、前年比での影響は長く続く。関連して、政府は 26年 12月から 27年2月使用分
（請求は翌月）に電気・ガス料金支援を再開する4。しかし、26年1-3月使用分にも補助を行っ
ていたため、前年比でみた物価上昇率の抑制効果は限定的なものとなるだろう（巻末図表「エ
ネルギー価格のコアCPIへの影響とその見通し」）。 
また、原油高は、エネルギー価格だけでなく、輸送費や原材料費の増加を通じて幅広い品目
の価格にも波及するため5、基調的な物価への上昇圧力となるとみられる。さらに、ドル円レー
トは足元で 150円/ドル台後半から 160円/ドル程度と、前年比で円安水準にある。原油価格の
上昇と併せて輸入物価への押し上げ圧力が強い状態であり、消費者物価への波及が見込まれ
る。 
賃上げによる人件費の増加分を販売価格に転嫁する動きも継続するだろう。26年度春闘にお
ける定期昇給相当込みの賃上げ率は、加重平均で5.01％と高水準を維持している6。さらに、26
年度の最低賃金の全国加重平均額も前年度比 5％程度引き上げられた7。こうした賃金上昇は、
サービス価格を中心に物価を押し上げると見込んでいる。 
これらの物価上昇圧力は27年度にかけても継続するだろう。他方、国会で審議中の飲食料
品の消費減税8が法案通り実施されると、コア CPI上昇率は前年比1.2％pt程度押し下げられる
ことから、27年度後半には前年比＋1％台まで縮小するだろう。

4 詳細は資源エネルギー庁「電気・ガス料金支援」（2026年11月20日閲覧）を参照。 
5 中村華奈子「原油高の国内への波及経路と価格転嫁率を踏まえた消費者物価への影響」（大和総研レポー
ト、2026年4月17日） 
6 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3年連続の 5％超え！ ～2026春季生活闘争 第7回（最終）回答集計結
果について～」（2026年7月3日） 
7 厚生労働省「令和 8年度 地域別最低賃金 答申状況」（2026年9月3日） 
8 国会で審議中の「租税及び社会保険料による負担の軽減のための飲食料品に係る消費税率の臨時的な引下げ
及び就業者負担軽減支援金の支給に関する法律」が成立すれば、軽減税率対象の飲食料品の消費税率は 2027
年 4月1日から2年間、現在の 8％から 1％へと引き下げられる。

### Page 1

ab 12 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
Accton Technology Corporation
Q4 GM to see strong recovery
Q3 GM  down 2.1pp QoQ on higher component cost
Accton reported Q326 EPS of NT$23.39, below UBS/Consensus estimates on softer
margins. Q326 sales of NT$120.7bn (up 26% QoQ) was already posted, and slightly
below UBSe as its  AI card business (Trainium 3) shifted to consignment model for certain
higher cost components from mid-August. Q326 GM of 17.6% was down 2.1ppts QoQ

due to   higher component cost and ~1.5 months of  consignment, and we believe this
should be temporary as its customer will reimburse them in the subsequent quarters. Q3
OPEX increased by  15% QoQ, primarily due to higher R&D expenses, resulted in OPM of
13.4% (-1.6ppts QoQ). We estimate data center switch accounted for ~37% of Q326
sales (vs 36% in  Q226), while accelerator card was ~50% of total sales (50% in Q226).
Q4  growth on track despite full quarter of consignment

Accton expects Q426F revenue to grow high-single digit % QoQ with potential upside
for low-teens % QoQ growth, subject to component supply (i.e. switch IC) and
customers' year-end inventory adjustment, despite a full quarter of component
consignment (vs 1.5 month in Q3). It sees robust demand from  cloud switch  with the
social media customer revenue contribution exceeded e-commerce customer from
Q326, which is likely to sustain into Q426F. It also noted the social media customer has

secured more switch chipsets from Marvell and Nvidia Spectrum, and continues to
negotiate with Broadcom for better allocation. Accton also sees better unit and content
growth for Trainium 3 as the customer ramps liquid-cooled design, which carry ~20%
higher ASP and better GM due to increased design complexity. We forecast Q426E sales
growth of 9% QoQ, while its GM should recover to 19.9% (+2.3 ppt QoQ) on more
favourable mix and its customer reimbursement on the Q3 prepaid component costs.

Aggressive capacity expansion to support mid-term  growth
Accton announced  US$226 mn (~NT$7.3 bn) new capex on 12 Nov. for capacity
expansion in Taiwan and Vietnam, as well as construction of new fab/cleanroom in
Malaysia and Singapore. It aims to fund the capex through internal cashflow, bank
loans,  and has announced a 10mn shares of rights issuance (~2% dilution) on 22 Oct.
We believe this will drive its overall capacity to grow 40-50% YoY in 2027E, supporting

overall 2027E revenue to grow ~35% YoY, driven by 1) further ramp of Trainium 3 with
higher adoption of liquid cooling, 2) sustained 800G switch demand and migration to
1.6T with new customer addition, 3) increasing contribution from Optical Switches.
Valuation: Reiterate Buy with NT$3,200 PT
We  fine tune our model and raise   2026-28E EPS by 0-2%. We retain  Buy but lower  PT to
NT$3,200 (from   NT$3,688) based on 22x  average 2027-28E PE (from 26x) on overall

market valuation compression. We believe recent concerns over switch IC  shortages and
GM pressure from higher component costs are overdone as we expect Accton's GM to
recover from Q426E. Accton will host its annual investor conference in late November.

Company Description
Founded in 1988, Accton specialises in ODM/OEM solutions for data center switches, AI
accelerator cards, enterprise/ODM switches, carrier access/telecom and others. Accton is one
of the largest vendors of white-box and OCP-compliant switches globally (by sales).

Leveraging deep expertise in high-speed switching, system integration and rapid time-to-
market, it has secured long-term partnerships with major cloud service providers.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Accton using PE methodology.
Key risks include: 1) weaker-than-expected demand for data center switches; 2) slower ramp
of 800G and 1.6T data center switches; 3) market share loss in the AI accelerator card
business; and 4) softer gross margin than expected.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Update
King Yuan Electronics Co Ltd  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Continuing capacity build with unchanged 2027 demand: The company mentioned
that AI demand remains strong and customers (especially the largest customer) are
still asking for more clean room build. 2027 demand is almost confirmed with no
changes over the past month.
Capacity allocation: US capacity will fully support the largest customer initially and
the capacity in Taiwan is fungible between different customers. For example, given

4Q26 weakness for consumer/smartphones, the company has shifted spare capacity
to meet other demand, including the testers from Advantest and Teradyne.
Margin should still be in the upward trend: The priority for the company now is to
have customers consign the equipment if they are asking for more capacity, lowering
the deprecation pressure. Also, given the tight capacity, the company would serve
the products with higher margin (generally AI demand and longer test time product)

Valuation Methodology and Risks
King Yuan Electronics Co Ltd (2449.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity of 9.8%, intermediate-term
growth rate of 10.5%, terminal growth rate of 3.0%, and cash dividend payout ratio of 65%.
Risks to Upside
n Rise in shipments for core customers such as MediaTek
n Strong AI demand from both GPU and ASIC
n Penetration of wafer sorting business
n Rise in IDM outsourcing to OSAT

Risks to Downside
n Overall non-AI demand remaining weak
n Significant testing market share loss
n Drop in shipments for core customers such as MediaTek

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

產品組合

股價相對大盤走勢

世芯-KY(3661 TT) Buy

新客戶加入，挹注 2027 年營運成長，建議 Buy。  
投資建議：世芯-KY 受惠北美 CSP 客戶 N3 AI 加速器量產，
2026 年營運大幅成長；展望 2027 年，除既有 N3 專案持續放量
外，美系新客戶約 10 億美元專案預計自 2Q27 起貢獻量產營收，
將推動營收及獲利進一步成長。中長期 N2 AI 加速器亦持續推
進，預計 2028 年進入量產，成為下一階段成長動能，預估 2026
年及 2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為 113.66 元及 193.02 元。惟隨量產
(COT)業務占比提高，營收成長未必同步帶動毛利率提升，且
CSP 持續提升自主設計能力，將是影響世芯-KY 長期商業模式
及評價的重要風險。我們認為上述風險主要影響中長期估值上
限，但未來 12 個月仍受惠 N3 放量及新客戶加入，基本面成長
趨勢明確。因此建議 Buy，目標價 4,635 元 (PER24X)。  
3Q26 稅後 EPS 37.67 元：北美 CSP 客戶之 N3 加速器晶片已
正式啟動量產出貨，世芯-KY 3Q26 營收大幅上揚至 260.51 億
元，QoQ+240.99%。晶圓、載板與先進封裝產能吃緊對 3Q26 營
收產生約 1 億美元之負面影響。受量產業務營收佔比拉升影響，
3Q26 毛利率下滑至 18.04%，較 2Q26 的 34.84%下滑 16.80 個百
分點。3Q26 稅後 EPS 37.67 元。 
取得美系新客戶訂單: 世芯-KY 確認已與知名美系大型巨頭簽
訂重大合約並展開執行，該案量約 10 億美元，合約已正式生效
並啟動，量產(MP)營收貢獻預計自 2Q27 後逐季增加，該專案確
定應用於 AI 領域。此新客戶為世芯-KY 此前未曾合作過之對
象，非中系且非新創企業，具備市場領導地位與龐大資本支出
規模，研判最具可能性之潛在對象為積極推進自研 AI 加速器之
北美雲端巨頭；世芯-KY 憑藉深厚後端物理設計與工程實力切
入合作，有效緩解客戶端工程資源吃緊之痛點，管理層明確指
出此案絕非一次性業務，而是世芯-KY 全新商業模式之開端，
該新客戶將躍升為世芯-KY 2027 年第二大客戶。

2 群益投顧 
  世芯-KY ESG：  
世芯電子身為全球 IC 設計服務領導廠商，為確保所有產品皆為符合環境安
全衛生規定之綠色產品，對供應鏈管理不遺餘力，與供應商合作將綠色環保
的政策及概念推廣到整個供應，並一起朝向減少地球環境之衝擊共同努力，
其中主要包括： 
有害物質管制 
符合世芯電子相關規範及國內、外法令等國際環境規範 
滿足世芯電子新外包商評鑑程序 
滿足世芯電子外包商管理程序 
滿足世芯電子環境管理物質管理程序 
歐盟危害物質限用指令 (EU RoHS Compliance) 
歐盟新化學品政策 (REACH) 
無鹵材料 (Halogen Free) 
綠色物料之承認管理 
供應商提出第三方對產品生產材料所作的環境物質分析報告或證明文件。 
綠色資訊平台之建置 
建構系統化之 IT 資料庫輔助管理系統，藉此平台，便利上下游廠商綠色零
件之交換，並帶動上游廠商綠色零件供應的能力，降低環保要求的衝擊程
度。 
建立環境管理系統 - 供應商必須擁有 ISO14001 環境管理系統或其他相關
環境管理系統相關驗證。 
無衝突礦產保證書-(Declaration of Metal Conflict-Free) 
衝突金屬係指來自剛果民主共和國衝突礦區之礦物，類別有鈮鉭鐵礦，錫
石，黑鎢礦與黃金等。這些礦物提煉成鉭(Ta)、錫(Sn)、鎢(W) 、鈷(Co) 和
金 (Au) 等，分別用於電子和其他產品。世芯電子強烈要求供應商提供的原
物料中不得間接或直接使用衝突礦產。 
企業社會責任 (CSR) - 確保供應商落實企業社會責任。 
業務連續性計劃 (BCP) 
供應商須有一套基於業務運行規律的流程，使組織在突發事件面前能夠迅
速作出反應，以確保關鍵業務功能可以持續，而不造成業務中斷或業務流程
本質的改變。 
綠色管理稽核 - 世芯-KY 每年持續對供應商進行稽核、改善及教育訓練，
經由系統化運作與管理，確保提供品質精良之卓越產品給客戶與消費者。

3 群益投顧 
N3 貢獻增加，3Q26 營收 260.51 億元，QoQ+240.99%，稅後淨利 32.51 億
元，QoQ+98.41%，稅後 EPS 37.67 元： 
北美 CSP 客戶之 N3 加速器晶片 已於 05/2026 正式啟動量產出貨，之後營
收逐月向上，世芯-KY 3Q26 營收大幅上揚至 260.51 億元，QoQ+240.99%。
在產能限制方面，晶圓、載板與先進封裝產能吃緊對 3Q26 營收產生約 1 億
美元之負面影響，若無此類供應限制，單季營收原可增加 1 億美元。資料中
心以外之其他應用在 3Q26 合計創造超過 1 億美元營收，其中中國車用 ADAS
需求維持強勁，客戶次世代晶片如期於 4Q26 進行流片(Tape-out)。  
3Q26 各應用佔公司營收比重，HPC 佔公司營收比重 87%，佔比仍為最高，
相較 2Q26 的 50%與 1Q26 的 70%大幅提升。消費性電子(Consumer)佔比降
至 8%，低於 2Q26 的 16%與 1Q26 的 17%。 Networking 佔 5%，較 2Q26 的
32%與 1Q26 的 10%下降。利基型(Niche，含超級電腦、虛擬貨幣、柏青哥
機台、醫療設備與監視系統)佔 0%，低於 2Q26 的 1%與 1Q26 的 3%。  
製程營收比重，N3/N2 佔公司營收比重大幅躍升至 80%，較 2Q26 的 47%與
1Q26 的 21%大幅增加，主因北美 CSP 客戶之 N3 加速器持續放量出貨。
7nm/5nm 佔比降至 15%，較 2Q26 的 40%下滑。16/12nm 佔比為 3%，較 2Q26
的 6%下降。28nm 及以下製程佔比為 1%，低於 2Q26 的 7%。  
地區營收分布隨著 N3 加速器放量出貨，地區分布再度出現結構性轉變：北
美地區佔比穩居第一大市場，達 84%(2Q26 為 51%， 1Q26 為 23%)。亞太地
區佔比降至 12%(2Q26 為 25%， 1Q26 為 47%)，車用晶片出貨維持穩健。其
他地區佔 4%(2Q26 為 17%， 1Q26 為 22%)。日本地區佔 1%(2Q26 為 7%，
1Q26 為 8%)。   
毛利率方面，3Q26 毛利率下滑至 18.04%，較 2Q26 的 34.84%下滑 16.80 個
百分點，為產品組合的最差情境，主因量產營收比重大幅拉升，致使委託設
計 (NRE)營收貢獻比例低於預期，加上數個專案之 NRE 認列產生遞延所致。
不過營業利益與稅後淨利同步創高。3Q26 世芯-KY 稅後淨利 32.51 億元，
QoQ+98.41%，稅後 EPS 37.67 元。 
N2 專案將在 2026 年底 Tape-Out：  
世芯-KY 承接之美系 CSP 次世代 N2 AI 加速器專案，將採用異質小晶片
(Heterogeneous Chiplet) 架構。晶片總面積維持在約 4 個光罩尺寸，但相較
於 N3 世代，N2 專案之運算晶粒(Compute Die) 配置、內部互連架構
(Interconnect)及封裝型式(2.5D/3D)均出現顯著改動。由於小晶片設計涉及
多個獨立晶( 包括主運算晶粒與 I/O 晶粒) ，各晶粒之開發週轉時間
(Turnaround Time)不同，專案將採取分階段進行多次定案(Tape-out)之模式。
2026 年底 Tape-out ， 2027 年底放量 
次世代 N2 加速器已進入最終實作階段，預計自 4Q26 開始展開流片，目標
於 4Q27 達成小量試產(Low volume production) ，並於 2028 年迎來量產放
量，推動世芯-KY 自 2028 年初邁入全新成長階段。N2 與 N3 在晶片設計複
雜度上有顯著差異，N3 主要為 SoC 設計結合 2.5D 封裝，而 N2 雖維持 2.5D
封裝架構，但已全面轉向 Chiplet 架構搭配全環繞閘極(GAA)製程技術。世
芯 -KY 負責 N2 晶片各個部分之實體設計並進行整合，其在 N2 專案之設計
參與深度與整體專案規模均顯著大於 N3。專案不依賴特定同業之第三方 IP，

### Page 4

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

4 群益投顧 
均採用通用型解決方案。在商業與生產模式上，世芯-KY 為 N2 之唯一實體
設計服務商，而在量產階段，客戶目前承諾僅會有客戶本身與世芯-KY 兩方
直接向台積電下單投片，世芯-KY 在 N2 之供應鏈地位與競爭優勢將較 N3
更為穩固。關於小晶片整合，包含 I/O Die 在內之晶片均由世芯-KY 統籌實
體設計，未來若因應 UALink 或 NVLink Fusion 等技術規格變更推出其他版
本之 I/O Die，世芯-KY 亦具備承接物理設計之先機。 
COT 合作模式短中期是有利世芯-KY，但長期要觀察 CSP 廠的全面自主設
計的進度： 
各大 CSP 為降低對傳統純 ASIC 廠商(如 Broadcom)高定價的依賴並實現
Cost-down，正全面轉向 COT 模式(客戶負責前端設計，世芯負責實體設計
與後端整合)。專案朝向「運算晶粒(Compute die)與  I/O 晶粒分工設計」發
展，世芯已與潛在客戶洽談負責運算晶粒並與外部 I/O 晶片整合的模式。
CSP 客戶轉向 COT 模式，本質上源於 3 項戰略考量： 
避免供應商鎖定(Vendor Lock-in) ：多數競爭對手採用垂直整合的封閉模式
(Captive Model)，從 IP、設計服務到封裝一條龍自主掌控，客戶一旦導入即
難以切換。CSP 客戶對此高度警惕，傾向採用第三方開放解決方案以保有供
應商多元化的彈性，降低單一供應商造成的議價劣勢與供應鏈風險。 
成本透明度與預算自主性：COT 模式下，客戶對晶圓、IP、設計服務及其他
供應鏈成本具有較高的掌握度，實際報酬及成本分攤方式則依個別專案合
約而定。相較於封閉模式下由供應商主導整體產品定價，COT 模式有助於
客戶提高成本控管能力與預算可預測性。 
規模擴張下的成本節省潛力： AI 晶片專案的出貨量與單位價值均遠高於傳
統晶片，隨著量產規模持續放大，COT 模式下透明且固定的利潤結構所帶
來的成本節省效益將等比例放大，對 CSP 客戶的財務影響不容忽視。 
然而，COT 模式對世芯-KY 的財務表現構成若干限制： 
COT 模式下的獲利空間取決於合約條件與服務範圍：COT 模式下，客戶通
常對專案成本及供應鏈有較高的掌握度，世芯-KY 的獲利則取決於其承接
的設計服務範圍、專案管理責任及個別合約的報酬安排。相較於自行掌握完
整產品定價與供應鏈的模式，若專案採成本加成或事先約定服務報酬的方
式，世芯-KY 透過晶片價格上漲或供應鏈成本變動擴大利潤的空間可能較
為有限。因此，未來毛利率及獲利表現，仍須觀察各專案的服務內容、合約
條件及營收組合變化。 
多方協作帶來的執行複雜度與供應鏈風險： COT 模式涉及晶圓廠、IP 供應
商、基板廠、封裝廠及測試廠等多個外部夥伴的協同整合，任一環節的產能
短缺或交期延誤均可能波及整體專案進度。世芯-KY 在 N7 世代曾因 ABF 基
板短缺而承受供應鏈壓力，此一歷史案例揭示開放生態系模式下供應鏈管
理的內在脆弱性。儘管公司已大幅增加供應商來源以分散風險，但多源管理
本身亦帶來額外的協調成本與品質控管挑戰。 
IP 自主能力缺失，技術話語權受限：世芯-KY 目前選擇與 Synopsys 等第三
方 IP 供應商合作，而非自行開發 IP。此策略雖降低研發資本支出，卻也意
味著公司在核心技術環節缺乏自主掌控能力，在特定 IP 出現供應限制或授
權條件變動時，可能面臨被動局面，長期而言亦限制公司在技術差異化上的
發展空間。

### Page 5

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

5 群益投顧 
規模成長不必然帶動利潤率同步提升：在固定利潤百分比的框架下，營收規
模的擴大主要反映的是晶圓與供應鏈成本的等比例放大，而非公司自身附
加價值的提升。換言之，世芯-KY 的獲利成長高度依賴出貨量的持續擴張，
而非利潤率的結構性改善，此一特性使公司的盈利模式更接近於量能驅動
型 (Volume-driven)，而非價值驅動型(Value-driven)。  
整體而言，世芯-KY 等 IC 設計服務公司的商業模式恰好能針對不同客戶的
能力缺口提供精準介入。無論客戶需求是後端實體設計落地、部分實體設計
協作、I/O Chiplet 的 IP 整合，乃至純量產委外，世芯均具備對應的服務能
量。此種高度彈性的模組化接案模式，使公司得以滲透不同成熟度的客戶群
體，有效降低拿單門檻。 
總而言之，CSP 晶片自研能力的持續演進是評估世芯-KY 長期投資價值不
可迴避的核心風險。從演進路徑來看，CSP 通常由依賴完整 ASIC 服務起步，
逐步過渡至主導架構設計但委外實體落地，最終邁向全面設計自主。世芯-
KY 目前所享有的設計服務需求，本質上是 CSP 處於此過渡階段所產生的結
構性紅利，一旦 CSP 設計能力全面成熟，世芯-KY 的角色將面臨從「策略
性設計夥伴」退化為「可替代供應鏈中介」的根本性風險，商業模式的可持
續性將受到挑戰。短期而言，N3 乃至 N2 先進製程實體設計的高度複雜性，
以及多代合作累積的工程默契，仍構成一定的切換成本緩衝。然而隨著 EDA
工具智能化持續壓低設計門檻，此一護城河的侵蝕速度是要警惕。群益認
為，世芯-KY 能否在當前紅利窗口期內成功深化 IP 能力、拓展先進封裝技
術佈局並實現客戶多元化，將是決定其長期商業模式能否升級躍遷的關鍵
觀察指標。 
N3 專案為 2026 年和 2027 年主要的營收貢獻： 
世芯-KY 目前有超過 60 項 FinFET 專案和 20 個以上 CoWoS 案子，平均每
年 20~30 new tape-out，且獲得多個 N3 專案，也有 N2 專案進行中。其中最
重要的專案是為北美 CSP 開發的 N3 AI 加速器(Trainium 3)。該專案在 4Q25
啟動，2Q26 末已進入量產。N5 完整的專案營收貢獻約 35 億美元，N3 的營
收貢獻會高很多，除主要客戶專案之外，世芯-KY 還贏得來自北美客戶的多
個 N3 專案，包括網路客戶以及北美和歐洲的主要網路設備廠商。 
現行 N3 晶片已符合各項測試標準且良率維持在合理水準，目前正全速出
貨，為 2026 年最大營收貢獻專案，亦將維持為 2027 年最大營收來源。管理
層預期當前世代專案將量產約 2 年時間，隨後由次世代接替。該專案預期於
4Q26 營收呈現小幅季增，並於 2027 年呈現大幅增長。世芯-KY 過去因營運
資金有限而在該專案初期未深化溝通，但後續成功自競爭對手處贏回此專
案，目前主要營運與產能限制已獲得解決，已做好承接更大出貨規模之準
備。 
取得美系新客戶訂單，預期於 2027 年躍升為世芯-KY 第二大客戶： 
世芯-KY 確認已與知名美系大型巨頭簽訂重大合約並展開執行，該專案整
體生命週期累計營收規模約 10 億美元，量產營收預計自 2Q27 開始貢獻，
後續逐季增加。已正式生效並啟動，量產(MP)營收貢獻預計自 2Q27 後逐季
維持季增趨勢，該專案確定應用於 AI 領域，推估採用台積電 N3 先進製程，
專案資產由雙方共同擁有。此新客戶為世芯-KY 此前未曾合作過之對象，非
中系且非新創企業，具備市場領導地位與龐大資本支出規模，市場推測可能

### Page 6

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 13 日

6 群益投顧 
為潛在對象為積極推進自研 AI 加速器之北美雲端巨頭 Meta 或 Microsoft；
世芯-KY 憑藉深厚後端物理設計與工程實力切入合作，有效緩解客戶端工
程資源吃緊之痛點，管理層明確指出此案絕非一次性業務，而是世芯-KY 全
新商業模式之開端，具備良好獲利能力且長期可持續，該新客戶自 2Q27 起
之量產放量將推動其躍升為世芯-KY 2027 年第二大客戶。 
2027 年 N3 案子貢獻持續，及美系新客戶加入，預估 2026 年和 2027 年稅後
EPS 分別為 113.66 元和 193.02 元： 
世芯-KY 2026 年 ~2028 年營收將會再大幅成長，因為 1)北美大客戶新世代
產品 N3 量產和下一代設計合作，公司預計其關鍵客戶在 2028 年前將有強
勁且可持續的營收貢獻；營收從 2Q26 開始顯著躍升。2)美系大型巨頭新客
戶的 10 億美元，量產(MP)營收貢獻預計自 2Q27 後逐季季增。3)車用業務
ADAS 方面，預計產品將成為從 2026 年開始的主要營收貢獻者之一。下一
代產品的開發已在進行中，確保營收連續性並為業務帶來強勁動能，直到
2028 年。 
受惠於 N3 AI加速器量產出貨小幅季增，疊加次世代 N2 專案展開流片(Tape-
out)與大型設計案啟動，4Q26 營收預估將達到 8.5~9 億美元區間，改寫單季
歷史新高。由於量產營收比重仍高達 80%以上，4Q26 毛利率預估將維持在
18~19.5%區間，公司預期 2026 年全年毛利率將達 20%以上。預估 2026 年
稅後 EPS 113.66 元。 
邁入 2027 年，1Q27 營收預估小幅拉回，自 2Q27 起，隨著規模達 10 億美
元的美國新大客戶量產營收正式啟動，營運將重啟逐季強勁增長動能，單季
營收將突破 10 億美元大關。3Q27 至 4Q27 在現有 N3 專案擴大出貨以及次
世代 N2 加速器於 4Q27 展開小量產(Low volume production) 挹注下，預估
2027 年稅後 EPS 193.02 元。 
2028 年次世代 N2 加速器進入量產： 
展望 2028 年，世芯-KY 大客戶的次世代 N2 加速器進入大規模量產階段，
由於 N2 專案轉向全環繞閘極(GAA)與 Chiplet 先進架構，世芯-KY 表示將
負責全晶片實體設計與整合，晶片價值量與專案規模將顯著超越 N3 世代。
因此管理層評估 2028 年營收具備再次翻倍之潛力，但群益先以保守情境預
估。 
在營運擴張過程中，世芯-KY 面臨兩大核心營運限制，分別為營運資金
(Working Capital)承載力以及高階研發人力瓶頸。在資金層面，管理層明確
指出 2028 年營收成長的瓶頸已非市場需求，而是資產負債表的承載能力。
由於雲端服務商(CSP)先進製程晶圓與先進封裝代工代採購金額極為龐大，
支撐 40 億美元以上營收需要巨額營運資金墊付晶圓廠貨款，公司資產負債
表結構將直接決定能承接的量產規模上限。在人力層面，單一先進製程大型
專案往往需要高達 150 位工程師投入，在 N3 出貨、N2 實作與新客戶專案
同步推進下，研發資源短缺成為主要限制，迫使公司採取高度篩選專案之策
略，僅承接具備實質商業營收之大型案件。為突破研發人力天花板，世芯-
KY 目前正積極對外擴編，預計 2026 年底員工人數將突破千人規模，並在
前端實作與驗證導入專屬內部 AI 訓練模型以大幅節省中段研發人力，但在
最終實體設計階段仍高度仰賴頂尖工程人力，高階人才擴充速度將是公司
長期承接新專案的關鍵決定因素。

10 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

3Q26 영업이익 1.8조, 핵심 하락 원인은 인센티브 
환율효과는 전분기 대비 -5,330억 작용했으나, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비의 급감 
(6,200억, -9,250억 qoq)으로 상쇄되었다 판단. 인센티브 비용 증가세는 -6,060억원의 영
향을 미쳤으며, 대부분 유럽지역 EV2 판매를 위한 딜러 인센티브에서 발생 추정. 
외생변수는 능력 밖의 일, EV 전략에 대해서는 장기성장에 무게 
중동 분쟁 지속, 환율 하락과 같은 외생변수의 부정적 영향을 제외한다면 단기 영업적 측면
의 실적 악재는 BEV 인센티브 지급기조의 변화가 없을 것이라는 점. 중장기 글로벌 BEV 
시장의 입지 강화를 단기 전사 수익성보다 중시하는 전략을 채택. 
마찬가지로, 4분기가 환율 효과의 본격화 
3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (QTD 1,338원) 효과로 환관련 
감익 본격화 예상. 즉, 판매보증비의 정상화 (qoq 반등)로 인해 수익성 추가 악화 가능성 존
재. 완성차 판매량의 경우 경쟁사 대비 견조했던 2Q-3Q26의 기조를 유지할 것이라 예상. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 129,000원으로 하향 
목표주가 하향 근거는 산정 기준을 FY27F로 변경함과 동시에 FY27F 자동차사업 NOPAT 
예상치 하향과 현대모비스 지분가치 하락. 밸류에이션상 저점에 도달했다는 의견은 FY27F 
EPS의 yoy 하락으로 설득력 약화. 중장기 BEV 시장 입지 강화에 대한 밸류에이션 프리미
엄 반영은 글로벌 전동화 속도에 달려 있음. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 외생변수의 개선, 2) 신
차 출시를 통한 상품성 강화, 3) BEV 시장 점유율 확대 이후 점진적 인센티브 축소, 4) 20조
원 수준의 순현금 기반 발생 가능한 사업적 결정.

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI 로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000 원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

Document 303: Brent at $77_bbl_ 3 calls for energy markets

### Page 1

Given the level of uncertainty in energy markets this year and
with little sign of a change going into next year, any call on
energy markets for 2027 is a low-conviction call. But here are
our 3 calls for energy markets for 2027
Warren Patterson
Oil ﬂows are recovering, but uncertainty remains
How to read our three calls for 2027
Source: ING
ING's base call: Brent to average
$77/bbl next year
Your privacy is important

### Page 2

Oil markets have been on a roller coaster this year given
events in the Middle East. However, we are going into year-
end with oil ﬂows from the region near pre-war levels despite
the lack of a deal between the US and Iran. While oil ﬂows are
normalising, the way oil is coming out of the region is
anything but normal, which is reﬂected in the surge in tanker
shipping rates, given the very ineﬃcient oil ﬂows.
Our base case assumes occasional ﬂare-ups between the US

and Iran, meaning a full normalisation of Persian Gulf
supplies remains some way oﬀ and the oil market is likely to
retain a degree of risk premium. However, provided there are
no signiﬁcant or prolonged disruptions, we expect the market
surplus to build over the course of 2027, leaving Brent to
average around $77/bbl. A deal between the US and Iran
would add further downside risk to this view.
Our risky call: OPEC will see more member
exits
We believe harmony within OPEC could be tested, with the

possibility of further members leaving the group. This follows the
UAE’s departure earlier this year. Post-Persian Gulf disruptions, any
breakdown or weakening of the group risks adding more supply to
the market, which would potentially leave further downside to our
base case price forecasts for Brent in 2027.
The most likely candidate to leave OPEC is Venezuela, with the US’s
growing inﬂuence over the Venezuelan oil industry following the
capture and arrest of President Maduro at the start of 2026. But we

believe that some Persian Gulf producers may also question their
membership, particularly if disagreements emerge over the
capacity numbers used by the group to set production targets. Iraq
has already made noise around production targets, and following
the exit of the UAE, some members may be looking at them
enviously, with the UAE pumping record volumes of crude since
leaving the bloc.

Our bold call: TTF to trade sub-€40/MWh
before the end of next year
European gas prices have strengthened signiﬁcantly in 2026. But

what goes up must come down, and we believe prices could fall
sharply next year. There are clearly plenty of assumptions here,
most importantly a normalisation of Persian Gulf LNG ﬂows in
early 2027. If that happens, we believe TTF could trade below
€40/MWh by end-2027.
On the demand side, higher prices are already driving some
industrial demand destruction, while we are deep in the gas-to-coal
switching range. Furthermore, El Niño could bring milder weather to

parts of Europe and East Asia. While far from guaranteed, this could
reduce heating demand in winter. So, we potentially come out of
the 2026/27 winter with the EU gas balance not as tight as many
fear, putting downward pressure on prices.
We also have signiﬁcant US LNG export capacity coming onto the
market, which should help with storage ahead of the 2027/28
winter. If the situation in the Persian Gulf normalises, we also have
new capacity starting up in Qatar.

Document 321: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 11

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

Document 351: Global Markets Outlook 2027_ Growing Pains

### Page 1

We have published our 2027 Macro Outlook: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks. We flesh 
out the Global Market implications and key asset views here, highlighting 10 
investment themes that are likely to dominate price action in 2027. More detailed 
outlooks for the individual asset classes are forthcoming.  
A maturing market cycle: Stable global growth and declining inflation make for 1.
a friendly macro baseline, but risks are to the downside as the market cycle ages. 
The AI investment impulse peaks: AI has contributed more to market gains 2.
than economic growth, so a bigger test for markets than the economy.  
Passing—or dropping—the baton: Equity gains move from AI infrastructure to 3.
other areas, but we see risks if tighter financial conditions thwart the transition 
or markets look further ahead. 
Energy—balancing risk and risk premium: Our baseline is for a gradual decline 4.
in energy prices, but an escalation-driven spike remains one of the largest 
near-term risks to markets. 
Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value: Higher running yield, more paths to 5.
relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher bond allocations, as supply shocks 
give way to demand stresses. 
A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies: Lower inflation and less 6.
tech exceptionalism should make for a softer Dollar through 2027, especially 
versus Asia’s revaluing currencies (JPY, CNY). 
Europe & UK—global shocks and local shifts: European markets are under 7.
pressure from global shocks and local concerns, which may persist until the 
French election, keeping EUR downside attractive. 
The stress of financing the boom: Record credit and equity issuance has been 8.
digestible so far, but with discounted pricing needed in credit, and further 
widening in US credit spreads is likely.

### Page 2

EM—resilience and diversification: The case for EM is exposure to high, diversified 9.
and resilient yields in EM fixed income and a regionally diversified earnings mix in EM
equities.
Growth risks and tail hedges: A benign baseline and tech has de-rated, but 10.
diversification benefits from bonds and commodities, and equity index volatility has
scope to rise further.
12 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 3

Markets Outlook 2027: Growing Pains

1. A maturing market cycle 
Stable growth, declining inflation make for a friendly macro backdrop. n
But risks are to the downside, and an ageing market cycle is facing more headwinds. n
Higher rates, wider spreads, and a peaking AI impulse mean the broad asset picture n
is more two-sided ... 
... and argues for more diversified allocations, including bonds and hedges. n
Our headline macro forecasts for 2027 are quite friendly: stable growth, declining 
inflation and peaking policy rates. But as the economic cycle extends, the market cycle is 
increasingly showing its age. For 2026, our view was that even though asset market 
valuations were running ahead of the macro, a positive cyclical backdrop including 
strong investment growth should dominate. While the Iran war has upended many 
forecasts, even without the energy shock the market is running into more conventional 
late-cycle headwinds. Real rates are higher, credit spreads are wider and volatility is 
picking up, especially under the surface. On top of this, the AI impulse, which has been a 
key support to markets through the shocks this year, looks poised to peak in the coming 
year. 
That is likely to prove a bigger headwind to the profit and asset market picture than 
growth, given its larger role in supporting equity markets. Markets have already moved 
some way towards reflecting that the extraordinary surge in profitability in the AI 
infrastructure complex is unlikely to be extrapolated. So a flattening in the gains there 
may be offset by gains further down the waterfall among AI users or other cyclical parts 
of the market, especially if energy prices and rates fall more. But that is a tricky 
balancing act, and further boosts to equity prices are likely to come alongside a higher 
base level of volatility. There are also risks that AI-related drawdowns tighten financial 
conditions more meaningfully, or that energy and policy relief do not arrive on time. 
That leaves the broader risk-asset picture looking more two-sided than it has for several 
years, arguing for more diversified and better-hedged portfolios. 
12 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 4

2. The AI investment impulse peaks 
The AI investment impulse peak is a bigger test for markets than the economy. n
That impulse has been a larger contributor to growth in market value and earnings n
than in GDP. 
Markets have already de-rated AI infrastructure beneficiaries. n
Valuation excesses are less visible, so the focus is on sustainability of record profits. n
Coming into 2026, we expected continued acceleration in AI investment to dominate 
valuation concerns in equities, but likely amid higher volatility and wider credit spreads.  
Although we expect solid AI investment growth to continue, our forecasts imply that the 
investment growth impulse—additional AI investment as a share of GDP—will peak in 
early 2027. From a macro perspective, this should be a manageable event. But it may be 
a bigger test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger 
contributor to growth in earnings and market value than to growth in GDP. Second, 
AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity gains, especially 
companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have made them more 
vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the point at which 
growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters, even if underlying 
growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through that peak allows 
markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and, as growth 
decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as we may 
be starting to see for the AI capex cycle. 
Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant de-rating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are 
most sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. Moreover, robust profits, and a rising share of

### Page 5

income going to listed companies, have helped keep both the imbalances and the deep 
valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this cycle is 
whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in profits, 
which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove persistent, 
especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation allowed during that 
tech cycle—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure 
on the cost of capital.

3. Passing—or dropping—the baton 
Strong levels of AI investment growth and profitability should limit the pressure. n
And the baton may be passed from AI infrastructure to other market areas. n
But we see risks of a larger aggregate shock if the market looks forward, or other n
areas cannot take up the slack. 
Tighter financial conditions or credit conditions could create a feedback loop. n
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition through the peak of 
the investment cycle will prove to be. The first is that although the investment impulse 
from AI looks poised to peak, growth in both AI investment and earnings should remain 
firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth 
to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a still-robust 11% in 2027. This may ultimately help to 
limit the de-rating of even the most directly affected areas and should reduce the 
impact on broader growth-sensitive assets, though it should also imply less relief in core 
rates pricing. The second is that the baton may be passed, at least to a degree, from the 
direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies that may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies that see some relief as the crowding out of capital

available to them eases. 
The risk is that what could be more of a rotation—and a flattening in the upward trend in 
asset markets—becomes a bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the 
market begins to look further forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 
on our forecasts); because the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place 
as quickly or as smoothly as needed; or because feedback loops from financial and 
credit conditions accelerate more quickly than in our baseline. We think the risk to 
markets is higher than the risk to the economic picture on each front, because the boom 
itself has had its largest impact there. For that reason, the most likely channel through 
which AI worries could lead to broader economic downside is through financial 
conditions themselves.

4. Energy—balancing risk and risk premium 
An escalation-driven oil spike remains one of the largest near-term risks to markets. n
Our baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices, but the hedge value of energy n
is still high. 
Downside tail to energy prices with clearer/faster resolution. n
Risk premium has fluctuated, so hedge value also varies over time. n
A potential oil price spike driven by another escalation in the war with Iran remains one 
of the biggest near-term risks to the markets and growth picture. Particularly after the 
fall in oil prices so far in November, pricing is vulnerable to any broadening in the conflict 
and new supply disruptions. We would expect that to hurt equities, support the Dollar 
against European currencies and put upward pressure on rates, all else equal. Europe, 
too, faces a precarious winter, given low storage levels in natural gas which leave the 
region vulnerable to risks from both supply and weather. If those risks are avoided, our 
baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices over the coming year even without full 
resolution of the conflict. Despite the ongoing war with Iran, oil market supply has 
largely been restored (even if refining capacity is limited), but risks of escalation and 
further disruptions have generally led the market to demand a fluctuating but 
meaningful risk premium above where we think the fundamental supply-demand picture 
would leave them.

### Page 7

Our baseline is a gradual recovery in supply and reduction in energy prices, leaving 
energy and core commodity returns more muted. But the risk of more serious 
disruptions is real, and energy prices could move significantly higher in those scenarios 
at a time when equities and, potentially, bonds could both be under pressure. The 
hedging value of the asset class remains high for that reason. The wrinkle is that the risk 
premium in oil has varied substantially and conditions the value of the hedge. When the 
risk premium falls, as it has done more recently, that hedging value is likely to be higher. 
When that risk premium is very high, as it was at the end of October, oil markets are 
more vulnerable to downside tails if news flow is better than expected, and outright 
longs (rather than options) have generally been more risky. The downside tail from faster 
progress towards resolution than we expect (not just in Iran, but in Ukraine too) is one of 
the clearest sources of upside risks for broader markets. In that scenario, energy prices 
could come down more quickly, supporting both bonds and equities.

5. Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value 
Rates moved up in 2026, on oil, hikes, AI spending and fiscal deficits. n
Central bank pricing now too hawkish relative to our baseline. n
Bonds should be a better hedge as supply shocks give way to demand stresses. n
Higher running yield, more paths to relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher n
bond allocations. 
Interest rates moved higher in 2026 after a low point on the eve of the Iran war. The 
resultant surge in energy prices and central bank pricing has been the primary catalyst 
across most DM and EM rates markets, but real rates, especially in the US, have 
continued to move higher as investment demand from the AI build-out interacts with 
sustained fiscal deficits. Despite that backdrop, we think that central bank pricing in 
many economies, including the US and the UK, is too hawkish relative to what is likely to 
be delivered. Even with the energy shock, the underlying inflation story is still fairly 
benign, which means that policy rates are likely to peak at lower levels relative to 
2023-2024 and reverse earlier. The risk to this view is that the energy shock extends 
further or inflation remains stickier and the labour market proves more resilient, as it did 
this year. 
Bonds have been a poor hedge in this environment of energy supply shocks, rate hikes

### Page 8

and real rate pressures from private and public sector spending. But we think the case 
for duration has shifted in a more constructive direction for several reasons. First, on a 
static basis, bond yields are now providing a good running yield and a decent spread to 
cash. Second, valuations are cheap (we see UST 10y yields at 4.6% by end-2027), 
positioning is underweight, and there are paths to some shallow rate relief from current 
levels under our benign baseline scenario, and scope for larger moves if AI spending 
decelerates more quickly. Third, the increase in yields has started to create stresses that 
indicate that a higher level of real rates may be difficult to sustain. Bonds are already 
starting to illustrate their value as a hedge against shocks of these types—with Bunds 
rallying as French sovereign spreads widened and USTs rallying as tech stocks came 
under pressure. Although energy spikes remain a near-term risk, we are more positive 
about bond allocations even after the recent rally, in part because of that hedging value.

6. A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies 
After tight ranges in 2026, lower inflation, energy prices make for a softer Dollar. n
But a bigger USD swing could come if US tech exceptionalism erodes. n
JPY is a more attractive hedge with ‘team Japan’ focused on limiting Yen weakness. n
CNY appreciation has further to run given undervaluation and a surging trade n
surplus.  
The broad Dollar is finishing 2026 on the strong side of tight ranges, buoyed by a sturdy 
US nominal growth backdrop, higher energy prices, and a shift to more hawkish Fed 
pricing. Under our baseline forecast, all three impulses should fade: alongside trend 
growth we expect inflation in the US to converge towards target, energy prices should 
decline as the Middle East disruptions gradually normalize and we expect the Fed to 
move from hikes to cuts as we head into late 2027 and early 2028. That should make for

### Page 9

a softer Dollar backdrop, and we have pencilled in a roughly 3% fall in the broad 
trade-weighted Dollar in 2027. 
But the real swing factor for FX is likely to be how the peak in AI-related tech spending 
affects US equity returns. Over the past several years, tech-related macro and market 
outperformance in the US has sucked in equity capital flows from the rest of the world 
and has been the primary factor supporting the Dollar’s premium valuation. Our 
baseline forecasts envisage a more even distribution of returns globally rather than US 
underperformance. But if US tech performance erodes meaningfully, that would likely 
come alongside some cyclical underperformance of the Dollar rather than a traditional 
‘safe haven’ bid, as we have already seen in short periods over the past couple of years. 
Instead, we would expect the undervalued Yen to prove a better haven in that scenario, 
given its typical sensitivity to equities and rates falling together. With Japanese policy 
authorities also now focused on limiting further Yen depreciation and encouraging 
inflows into Japanese assets, we think that the Yen should increasingly be a more 
attractive hedge to pro-cyclical portfolios. We also see scope for the Yuan to continue to 
appreciate gradually as China’s trade surplus keeps growing, and forward markets 
underestimate how far this process can run given the deep undervaluation starting 
point. While domestic demand is also very weak, we have argued that China needs both 
currency appreciation and expansionary policy, two instruments to hit two targets, to 
move towards better external and internal balance.

7. Europe & UK—global shocks and local shifts 
European markets have been under pressure from energy ToT, higher global yields n
and sovereign stresses. 
Even if global shocks abate, local concerns still persist until the French election, n
keeping EUR downside attractive.

### Page 10

UK equities are resilient and defensive, Sterling has continued to strengthen. n
UK Gilts may be clearer outperformers given their highest beta to oil prices. n
European macro markets have had a tough year, primarily reflecting a negative terms of
trade shock, rate tightening without an offsetting investment spending boom, and most
recently the reappearance of sovereign stresses, especially in France. Even with the
repricing, there are still challenges: the energy risks are likely to be most acute going

through the winter, and the underlying fiscal issues in France are unlikely to be
adequately addressed until the French election in the Spring of 2027. As a result, using
the Euro as a funder for other crosses has been a popular position, and the skew of risks
and low carry continue to make downside optionality attractive for the near term. While
Europe remains hostage to global shocks, the main paths to relief also run through those
global channels—energy relief would be most helpful, as would lower yields in the rest of

core markets. Together they would also lessen the immediate pressure on fiscal budgets,
even though the underlying primary deficit would still need to be addressed. Any
slowdown in AI-related spending may also show European assets in a better relative light
given a much smaller share of tech in the region’s equity indices.
In those scenarios, the UK may shine even brighter. Gilts have had the highest beta to oil
prices, so a reduction in those pressures may provide the most relief to UK rates and to

the fiscal picture there. UK equity indices also have less tech exposure than the US
(although they do embed significant energy exposure), and so may also prove more
defensive in an AI-related spending slowdown and any associated market pressures. And
while the currency may also benefit from a softer Dollar backdrop, it is already the most
overvalued currency within our G10 coverage. Much has gone right for the Pound in
recent months—a smooth transition to a new Labour government, a ‘do-no-harm’

budget delivery, the BoE shifting to hikes, and encouraging talk of a closer settlement
with the EU. But we think further gains are likely to be more challenging, with our
monetary policy forecasts most dovish to market pricing in the UK.

Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 11

8. The stress of financing the boom 
AI investment financing has driven credit and equity issuance to cycle records. n
This has been digestible so far, but with discounted pricing needed in credit that has n
led spreads wider. 
A further widening in US spreads looks likely and a higher chance of meaningful n
widening than meaningful tightening. 
Europe offers more risk premium; mortgages and structured credit give some refuge n
from corporate supply. 
Credit and equity issuance has been hitting fresh cycle records, and the financing needs 
of the AI investment boom mean that those needs are likely to build further in 2027. So 
far, that issuance has—for the most part—been digestible, albeit with discounted 
pricing, and the credit underperformance that we expected in 2026 because of these 
dynamics has only really become clear in the last few months. Balance sheets of the 
major borrowers are strong and while free cash flow positions for the hyperscalers have 
deteriorated sharply, the overall corporate sector is much more balanced. We expect 
continued bifurcation between AI-related issuers and “everything else”, with episodic 
indigestion of jumbo AI debt financing leading to wider spreads for AI-related sectors.  
We expect further widening in index-level credit spreads in 2027, though in our base 
case the scope is fairly modest. The risks around that view are skewed though. It is hard 
to see scenarios where US credit spreads tighten sharply (there is more scope for 
tightening in Europe where the risk premium has increased more), and easier to see how 
spreads could move wider, particularly if economic conditions deteriorate more than we 
expect or if investors become more uncomfortable about the profitability of the AI 
investment boom. We expect modest US HY underperformance (decompression) in our

### Page 12

baseline, but the risk is that problems in the left tail of weaker borrowers create more 
turbulent conditions for broader financing. Mortgages and structured credit markets 
may offer some refuge from the heavy corporate supply. From 1998 to 2000, there was 
sustained widening in credit spreads and substantial decoupling between credit and 
equity markets. Without the large capital inflows and goods price deflation from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998, we think that kind of divergence will be harder now.

9. EM—resilience and diversification 
2026 is one more year when EM assets have shown resilience/outperformance in the n
face of external shocks. 
But the flip side is less compelling valuations and lower carry, despite friendly macro. n
So the case for EM investing is exposure to high, diversified and resilient yields in EM n
fixed income ... 
... and a regional and sectorally diversified earnings mix in EM equities.  n
EM assets have again demonstrated impressive resilience in 2026 in the face of shocks 
from high energy prices and the spikes in core rates. EM rates have outperformed core 
rates, EM FX total returns are outpacing G9 and EM equities are on track for a narrower 
but still double-digit year after 30% gains in 2025. The upshot of this resilience and 
outperformance is that in many places valuations are less compelling than before. EM 
local rate spreads are tight relative to core rates, EM credit spreads are the tightest in 
years despite attractive all-in yields, and EM FX carry and valuations are firmly 
middle-of-the-road. 
Even though the baseline macro backdrop looks friendly for EM investing and 
carry-harvesting in 2027, less attractive valuations and carry levels mean that it is about 
gaining exposure to high diversified yields in both hard and local currency fixed income. 
In many cases, such as South Africa, Hungary, Colombia and India, those higher yields 
come with improving fiscal and structural reform stories. Likewise, in the case of EM 
equities, while the major markets are not expensive, much of the value in the headline 
index is an artefact of the surge in AI earnings that will be hard to repeat. Rather, the 
case for EM equity length is also about gaining exposure to a diversified earnings mix.

### Page 13

The EM equity index, especially the less concentrated, equal-weighted flavours, provide 
exposure not just to the tech and AI complex in North Asia, but also to cyclical and 
commodity-related growth in South Africa and LatAm, and to larger domestic demand 
stories such as India. And if we see a larger move lower in core rates or the Dollar than 
our baseline expectations, EM local assets provide upside convexity that is harder to 
replicate elsewhere.

10. Growth risks and tail hedges 
A benign baseline, but growth risks now skew to the downside. n
Diversification benefits from bonds, commodities and equity selection. n
Equity index volatility has scope to rise further. n
Downside in rates, USD/JPY, upside in energy and protection in equity options. n
With a steady economic backdrop, our baseline forecasts for 2027 are for positive 
returns across equities and bonds. But with a maturing market cycle and significant 
uncertainty around core drivers of the outlook, we continue to emphasize the value of 
diversification and hedges to protect against key risks. Some of those risks—from higher 
oil prices or sticky core inflation—are likely to push rates higher. But a growing number—
a sharper reversal in the AI boom, cybersecurity fears, AI-related labour-shedding or 
financial stresses—would push both growth and rate expectations lower. The further we 
move into 2027, the more likely it is that the key risks are positive for bonds, as the 
threats from central bank hikes, energy price spikes and the AI investment tailwind 
recede. Diversification remains valuable in this environment. As we have said, the value 
of bonds as a diversifying asset is returning as the skew of risks shifts. And 
commodities—especially energy—continue to play a hedging role for portfolios, 
particularly when implied risk premia fall as they have recently.  
Alongside diversification, direct hedges are worth considering. Positioning for lower 
rates in the US and Japan, or for jointly lower rates and equities are potential ways to 
protect against deeper downside growth tails. In FX, USD/JPY downside may play a 
similar role, given that it generally performs well when rates and equities are both falling

Document 357: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP, household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.
